8.1 — Identity Foundation

Who Kevin Is:
The Identity Statement

✦ Kevin's Daily Identity Statement

"I am Kevin — a disciplined, high-performing athlete and student. I choose difficulty daily because it is building the person I am becoming. I train with purpose, eat to fuel performance, and act with intention. I do not waste my potential."

Read this aloud every morning. Research shows vocalising identity statements increases follow-through 2–3× vs reading silently. Takes 20 seconds. Never skip it.

Language Upgrades

  • "I should" → "I choose to." Restores agency and personal ownership.
  • "I can't" → "I don't." Identity statement vs excuse. Massive psychological difference.
  • "I'll try" → "I will." Try = permission to fail without consequence.
  • "I failed" → "I learned." Data vs identity collapse.
  • "I'm tired" → "I'm getting stronger." Discomfort = adaptation.

The Decision Filter

  • Does this serve who I'm becoming? Your 20-year-old self is watching every choice you make right now.
  • Is this easy or right? Most bad decisions are easy now, costly later. Most good decisions are uncomfortable now, rewarding later.
  • Am I acting from values or mood? Mood is unreliable. Values are consistent.
  • Will I be glad I did this tomorrow morning? Simple gut check that bypasses all rationalization.

Non-Negotiable Standards

  • Be the hardest worker in every room — not the loudest
  • Do what you say you'll do, when you say you'll do it
  • Never complain without a proposed solution
  • Be on time. Always. No exceptions.
  • Hold yourself to a higher standard than anyone else holds you to
  • Clean up after yourself immediately — environment reflects identity
8.2 — Mental Toughness

Building an
Unbreakable Mind

⚡
The core principle: Mental toughness is not a personality trait — it is a trainable skill built through repeated voluntary discomfort. You build it exactly like muscle: progressive overload on hard situations. Cold showers, hard training sessions, studying when distracted, eating when not hungry — every time you choose the harder right, you are training your mind.

The 4 Pillars

  • Control: Focus exclusively on what you can control: effort, preparation, attitude, recovery. Let go completely of what you cannot: results, other people's behaviour, referee decisions, luck. This is not passive — it is strategic energy allocation.
  • Commitment: Show up when you don't feel like it. The training session you do when you least want to is worth 3× one done fully motivated. Motivation is unreliable. Commitment is a system.
  • Challenge: Hard situations are opportunities to prove your standard. When training is brutal: "This is exactly where I get better." Reframe pressure as proof you're in important situations.
  • Confidence: Built through action, not affirmations. Do hard things consistently. That is the only real source of self-belief. Confidence from action is durable. From affirmations it is fragile.

Daily Mental Toughness Training

  • Cold shower daily (non-negotiable): Not for the physical benefit — for the daily act of choosing discomfort over comfort. Every morning you do it, you prove you control your impulses. This transfers everywhere.
  • No snooze, ever: The first decision of every day matters more than any individual workout. Win the first moment or lose the day's momentum.
  • One voluntary discomfort daily: Difficult conversation, sitting through boredom without a phone, finishing a set when you want to stop, eating when not hungry. Each one is a mental rep.
  • The 5-second rule: When you know you should do something — count 5-4-3-2-1 and move immediately. Your brain builds excuses for delay within 5 seconds. Never give it time.
  • Finish what you start: Never leave a session, meal, study block, or chapter unfinished without a deliberate reason. Incompletion trains your brain to accept incompletion as normal.
8.3 — Dopamine Control

Reclaiming Your
Brain from the Phone

📱
The core problem: Social media is engineered by billion-dollar companies using unpredictable reward schedules — identical to slot machines. This hijacks your dopamine circuits and collapses your baseline enjoyment of ordinary activities. Your capacity for sustained effort in studying, training, and learning is directly inversely correlated to your scrolling habit.

The 7 Non-Negotiable Phone Rules

  • Phone charged in kitchen overnight. Not bedroom. Not silent in bedroom — another room entirely. Prevents midnight and morning spiral.
  • No phone 60 minutes after waking. Your morning neurochemistry sets the tone for the entire day. Do not let Instagram set it.
  • Phone in bag during school. Never in pocket. Visible phone reduces cognitive performance even untouched.
  • Designated windows: 12:30pm and 5:30pm, 20 min max each. Set a timer. Everything can wait.
  • Greyscale mode always on. Settings → Accessibility → Colour Filters → Greyscale. Colour is engineered to stimulate. Greyscale reduces phone appeal 30–40% immediately.
  • Delete all social apps except 2. Which two are genuinely valuable? Keep those. Delete the rest. Browser access only creates useful friction.
  • Screen time: 90 min/day total. Set with parent passcode. Non-negotiable.

30-Day Recalibration Protocol

Your brain will protest in the first 3–10 days. The restlessness and boredom are withdrawal — proof the dependency was real. Push through. By day 14, your baseline enjoyment of ordinary activities measurably increases.

  • Week 1: Delete 3 social apps. Phone in kitchen at night. Note the anxiety — this is data.
  • Week 2: Add phone-free morning (60 min). Set screen time limits. No phone at meals.
  • Week 3: Designated windows only. Greyscale on. Track screen time weekly.
  • Week 4: 90-min/day limit with passcode. System installed. Maintain it.
Replace scrolling with high-value dopamine: Exercise, cold shower, reading, real conversation, creative work — these raise your baseline. Scrolling lowers it.
The replacement habit: When the urge to scroll hits — do 10 push-ups instead. After 2–3 weeks this becomes automatic. You associate the urge with strength, not surrender.
8.3.5 — Kevin's Phone Recovery Protocol

From 4-5 Hours Daily
To Under 90 Minutes

You're spending 4-5 hours daily on your phone. This is your biggest distraction. It's killing your training progress, your study focus, your sleep, and your chances with that girl at church. This protocol is designed specifically for your situation.

⚠️
Reality check: 4-5 hours daily = 28-35 hours per week = 1,456-1,825 hours per year. That's 60-76 full days of your life every year spent scrolling. In 5 years, that's 300-380 days — AN ENTIRE YEAR of your life gone. What could you achieve with that time? Getting jacked. Getting that girl. Accelerating in maths. Building a business. Your phone is stealing your future.

🎯 Kevin's 60-Day Phone Recovery Protocol

This is not a 30-day gentle transition. This is aggressive intervention. You're losing 4-5 hours daily. That's an emergency.

Days 1-7: The Shock Phase

  • Delete ALL social apps except 1. Choose the one that actually adds value. Delete the rest cold turkey.
  • Greyscale mode ON. Settings → Accessibility → Colour Filters → Greyscale. Keep it on 24/7.
  • Phone in kitchen at night. No exceptions. Buy an alarm clock if needed.
  • No phone 90 minutes after waking. Wake up at 7am, phone allowed at 8:30am.
  • Set screen time limit to 3 hours/day. Parent passcode required. Non-negotiable.
  • Track every minute. Use Screen Time app. Review daily before bed.

Days 8-14: The Withdrawal Phase

  • Reduce screen time to 2.5 hours/day. You will feel restless. That's withdrawal. Push through.
  • No phone during meals. Breakfast, lunch, dinner. Phone in another room.
  • Designated windows only: 12:30-1:00pm (recess) and 5:30-6:00pm (post-gym). 30 min each.
  • Replace urge with push-ups. Every time you want to scroll, do 10 push-ups immediately.
  • Journal the withdrawal. Write how you feel each night. This is data.

Days 15-30: The Rebuilding Phase

  • Reduce screen time to 2 hours/day. You're halfway there.
  • Add phone-free study blocks. 45 minutes phone-free before every study session.
  • No phone in bedroom ever. Not even for alarms. Buy a cheap alarm clock.
  • Replace social with reading. 15 minutes reading for every 15 minutes phone use.
  • Weekend challenge: 4 hours max total on weekends. Track it.

Days 31-45: The Discipline Phase

  • Reduce screen time to 1.5 hours/day. You're approaching elite territory.
  • Delete 1 more app. Down to 0 social apps or 1 max.
  • Phone in bag at school. Never in pocket. Never visible.
  • 30-day streak challenge: Can you go 30 days with under 90 min daily?
  • Reward yourself: If you hit 90 min for 7 days straight, buy yourself something training-related.

Days 46-60: The Elite Phase

  • Target: 90 minutes/day maximum. This is your new normal.
  • Designated windows: 12:30-1:00pm and 5:30-6:00pm only. 30 min each. That's it.
  • No phone 60 minutes before bed. Sleep quality will improve massively.
  • No phone first 60 minutes of morning. Morning routine first, phone second.
  • Maintenance mode: If you exceed 90 min, reset to Day 1. No excuses.

Beyond 60 Days: The New Normal

  • 90 min/day is your ceiling. Never exceed it without deliberate reason.
  • Weekly review: Check Screen Time every Sunday. Adjust if needed.
  • Phone as tool, not addiction: Use it for what you need. Put it away.
  • Notice the difference: Better focus, better training, better sleep, better social confidence.
  • Never go back: You know what 4-5 hours feels like. Never return to that.

🎮 Gaming & Doomscrolling: Specific Protocols

Your biggest distractions are phone, laptop, games, and doomscrolling. Here's how to handle each:

  • Gaming: Max 1 hour on weekends. None on school nights. If you can't control it, uninstall.
  • Laptop distractions: Use website blockers (Freedom, Cold Turkey). Block social media, gaming sites, YouTube.
  • Doomscrolling: Set a 15-minute timer when you open social media. When timer goes off, close it immediately.
  • YouTube: Only watch for specific learning (training tutorials, study content). No mindless browsing.
  • TikTok/Reels: Delete these apps entirely. They're engineered for addiction. No exceptions.
  • Browser extensions: Install "News Feed Eradicator" for social media. Removes the feed, shows motivational quotes instead.
  • Phone placement: When at home, phone in kitchen. Never in bedroom. Never at desk while studying.
  • Replacement activities: When bored, do push-ups, read, journal, meal prep, plan training. Never default to phone.
  • Accountability: Tell your parents about your 90 min goal. Ask them to check your Screen Time weekly.
  • Emergency protocol: If you relapse and spend 4+ hours in a day, don't shame yourself. Reset to Day 1. Start over immediately.

💡 What You Gain From This

Going from 4-5 hours to 90 minutes daily saves you 3-3.5 hours per day. That's 21-24.5 hours per week. Here's what you can do with that time:

  • Extra training time: 3 hours more per week = faster gains, better technique, more progress
  • Better study focus: Phone-free study = deeper learning, better grades in accelerated maths
  • Better sleep: No blue light before bed = 8-9 hours quality sleep = faster muscle growth
  • Social confidence: Less time comparing yourself online = more confidence in real life = better with girls
  • Mental clarity: Less dopamine chaos = clearer thinking, better decision-making, stronger discipline
  • Time with God: Less scrolling = more time for prayer, reading scripture, reflecting on faith
✦
The girl at church: She's not going to be impressed by a guy who spends 4-5 hours daily scrolling. She's going to be impressed by a guy who has discipline, who's focused, who's working on himself. Your phone addiction is literally standing between you and her. Fix this, and everything else becomes easier.
8.3.8 — Daily Journal System

Kevin's Daily
Mindset Journal

📝 The Daily Journal Protocol

Every evening, spend 10-15 minutes journaling. This is not optional — it's a non-negotiable part of your mindset training. The journal tracks your progress, builds self-awareness, and creates accountability.

  • Day Rating (1-10): Rate your overall day. Be honest. 1 = terrible, 10 = perfect. This tracks patterns over time.
  • Happiness Rating (1-10): Rate your happiness level independent of performance. This separates achievement from wellbeing.
  • Daily Narrative: Write what happened. School, training, friends, challenges. Get it all out.
  • Gratitude: 3 things you're grateful for. Shifts focus from what's wrong to what's right.
  • Faith & Confession: Prayer, scripture reading, confession of struggles. Connect with your faith.

✅ The 16 Daily Habits Checklist

Track these 16 habits daily. Each completed habit adds points to your daily score. A perfect day = 100 points.

  • No Masturbation: +15 points if true (critical habit)
  • Exercise: +2.7 points
  • Prayer: +2.7 points
  • Reading: +2.7 points
  • Healthy Eating: +2.7 points
  • Sleep Early: +2.7 points
  • Drink 2L Water: +2.7 points
  • Screen Time Under 3h: +2.7 points
  • No Screen Wake/Sleep: +2.7 points
  • Wake Early: +2.7 points
  • Read Bible: +2.7 points
  • Diary Writing: +2.7 points (automatic points)
  • No Arguments Parents: +1.25 points
  • Shower: +1.25 points
  • Sunlight & Stretch: +1.25 points
  • School Focus: +1.25 points

📊 Score Calculation System

Your daily score is calculated automatically based on your ratings and habits:

  • Day Rating: Rating × 3.5 (max 35 points)
  • Happiness: Happiness × 1.5 (max 15 points)
  • No Masturbation: +15 points (critical)
  • 11 Essential Habits: +2.7 points each (max 30 points)
  • 4 Bonus Habits: +1.25 points each (max 5 points)
  • Total: Score clamped 0-100, rounded
Why this matters: Tracking creates accountability. Seeing your score motivates improvement. Patterns emerge over weeks and months. This data drives your growth.
8.4 — Focus & Consistency

Deep Focus &
The Consistency System

The Pre-Focus Protocol (3 min)

  • Clear desk entirely. Only current subject visible. Clutter competes for attention even when not consciously noticed.
  • Phone in another room. Not silent — another room. Visible phone reduces cognitive capacity by 10% even untouched.
  • Write one sentence: "I am studying [topic] because [outcome]." Connects action to purpose and activates prefrontal engagement.
  • Water bottle on desk. Dehydration of just 1–2% impairs cognition by up to 20%.
  • 5 box breaths. 4 counts in, 4 hold, 4 out, 4 hold. Switches nervous system to focused state in under 2 minutes.

The Consistency Rules

  • 1% better daily. 1.01^365 = 37.8× improvement in one year. Not elite — just consistently 1% better. This is the only maths that matters in long-term development.
  • Never miss twice rule. Missing one day is an accident. Missing two days in a row is starting a new habit. Return to schedule immediately after any miss.
  • Make decisions in advance. What you will eat, train, study — decided the night before. In-the-moment decisions are always less disciplined than advance decisions.
  • Weekly Sunday review. What went well? What didn't? What's the single thing that would have made this week better? 10 minutes. Every week without exception.
  • Track habits visually. Streaks are motivating. Use a paper habit tracker on your wall or a phone app. Make progress visible.
  • Discipline = freedom. The more disciplined your training, eating, studying, and sleeping — the more free time, energy, and options you have. Undisciplined people spend their freedom reacting to crises they created.
8.5 — Faith-Based Motivation

Faith as the Foundation
of Your Discipline

Your faith in Christ is one of your biggest motivators. This isn't just spiritual — it's practical. Faith provides meaning, purpose, and resilience that secular motivation cannot match. Here's how to integrate your faith into your daily pursuit of excellence.

✦
The biblical foundation: "Whatever you do, work at it with all your heart, as working for the Lord, not for human masters." (Colossians 3:23). Your training, your study, your discipline — all of it can be an act of worship. This changes everything. You're not just getting jacked for yourself. You're stewarding the body God gave you.

🙏 Integrating Faith into Daily Discipline

Your faith should inform every area of your life. Here's how:

  • Training as stewardship: Your body is a temple (1 Corinthians 6:19). Training isn't vanity — it's stewardship. You're taking care of what God entrusted to you.
  • Study as excellence: "Whatever your hand finds to do, do it with all your might" (Ecclesiastes 9:10). Your studies are an opportunity to develop the mind God gave you.
  • Discipline as worship: Every early morning, every hard workout, every focused study session — these are acts of obedience to the call to excellence.
  • Prayer before challenges: Before big tests, before hard training sessions, before difficult conversations — pray. Ask for strength, wisdom, peace.
  • Gratitude for ability: Thank God for the ability to train, to study, to improve. Not everyone has these opportunities.
  • Scripture for motivation: Find verses that speak to your goals. Memorize them. Recite them when motivation wanes.
  • Church as community: Your church community (altar serving, soccer, running club) is your support system. Lean on it.
  • Witness through excellence: Your discipline and improvement testify to God's work in your life. People notice excellence.
  • Serving others: Use your growing strength and capability to serve. Help others. This keeps pride in check.
  • Humility in success: Remember that every ability and opportunity comes from God. Stay humble. Give credit where it's due.

📖 Kevin's Faith-Based Motivation Verses

Memorize these. Recite them when you need strength:

  • 1 Corinthians 9:24-27: "Do you not know that in a race all the runners run, but only one gets the prize? Run in such a way as to get the prize."
  • Philippians 4:13: "I can do all this through him who gives me strength."
  • Proverbs 21:5: "The plans of the diligent lead surely to abundance, but everyone who is hasty comes only to poverty."
  • Colossians 3:23: "Whatever you do, work at it with all your heart, as working for the Lord, not for human masters."
  • 1 Timothy 4:8: "For physical training is of some value, but godliness has value for all things."
  • James 1:2-4: "Consider it pure joy whenever you face trials of many kinds, because you know that the testing of your faith produces perseverance."
  • Romans 5:3-4: "We also glory in our sufferings, because we know that suffering produces perseverance; perseverance, character; and character, hope."
  • Galatians 6:9: "Let us not become weary in doing good, for at the proper time we will reap a harvest if we do not give up."
  • Psalm 37:5: "Commit your way to the Lord; trust in him and he will do this."
  • Joshua 1:9: "Be strong and courageous. Do not be afraid; do not be discouraged, for the Lord your God will be with you wherever you go."

🎯 Faith-Informed Daily Routine

Integrate faith practices into your existing routine:

  • Morning prayer (2 min): Before you start your day, pray. Thank God for the day ahead. Ask for strength and wisdom. Commit your work to Him.
  • Pre-training prayer (30 sec): Before hard workouts, pray for safety and strength. "God, help me push through this. Let this build character."
  • Pre-exam prayer (30 sec): Before tests, pray for clarity and calm. "God, help me focus. Let me use the mind you gave me."
  • Meals: Thank God for your food. Remember that not everyone has access to the nutrition you do.
  • Evening prayer (2 min): Review your day. Thank God for wins. Ask for forgiveness for failures. Commit to improvement tomorrow.
  • Sunday Mass: Attend every Sunday. This is your weekly reset. Receive grace. Reconnect with community.

💪 When Motivation Fails — Faith Endures

Secular motivation is based on feelings, which are unreliable. Faith is based on conviction, which endures:

  • When you don't feel like training: Your body is a temple. Discipline is stewardship. Do it anyway.
  • When you're tired: God gives strength to the weary. Trust that. Push through.
  • When you fail: God's grace covers your failures. Learn, forgive yourself, move forward.
  • When you're discouraged: This is temporary. God's plan is bigger than your current struggle.
  • When you're tempted: Pray for strength. Remove yourself from temptation. Don't rely on willpower alone.
  • When you succeed: Give glory to God. Stay humble. Remember where your abilities come from.
  • When you're proud: Pride comes before the fall. Stay grounded. Remember you're not self-made.
  • When you're anxious: "Do not be anxious about anything" (Philippians 4:6). Pray instead of worrying.
  • When you're lonely: God is with you always. You're never truly alone.
  • When you're uncertain: Trust God's plan. "For I know the plans I have for you" (Jeremiah 29:11).
✦
The ultimate motivation: You're not just getting jacked for yourself. You're not just getting good grades for yourself. You're developing the person God created you to be — strong, capable, disciplined, humble, kind. Every rep, every study session, every act of discipline is part of becoming who He designed you to be. That purpose will sustain you when nothing else will.
✦

The Mind Comes First.
Everything Else Follows.

Every physical, athletic, and academic improvement in this OS depends on one thing: your ability to choose the long-term right over the short-term easy. That is the entire game. Train your mind to make that choice — repeatedly, under pressure, every single day.

  • Phone mastery: Deep work sessions 90+ min without urge to check. Phone as tool, not addiction.

Elite Operation — Operate at Your Ceiling

Athletic Ceiling

  • Body weight: 72–78 kg. Full muscular development. Complete transformation from 50kg Day 1.
  • Running: 5K sub-19 min / 10K sub-40 min. 5 years of progressive training. Legitimate competitive club times.
  • Sprint: 100m under 11.5 seconds. Elite junior territory with proper long-term development.
  • Swimming: 400m under 5:30. Open water 1km comfortable. Club competitive level.
  • Cycling: Century ride (100km) completed. Multi-day touring capable.
  • Sprint triathlon competitive finisher: Complete multi-sport capability.

Life Architecture

  • Academic: University or career from strength. Multiple good options, chosen deliberately.
  • Character: 5 years of daily discipline, voluntary discomfort, standards-based living. A young man of genuine reliability and presence.
  • Health trajectory: Habits installed at 15 — sleep, nutrition, training, controlled screen time — create a health trajectory that diverges massively from peers over decades.
  • Financial: Budget managed intentionally. Saving, earning, spending deliberately.

G0 — The Fortnight Pattern

Your A/B Training
Cycle Explained

You train gym 3× per week. Because A and B alternate, across a fortnight you automatically balance the sessions. Week A gets 2× Day A, 1× Day B. Week B gets 2× Day B, 1× Day A. Perfect balance over the fortnight.

📅 Week A Pattern

MON
🏋️ Day APush + Squat dominant. Chest, shoulders, quads, triceps.
TUE
🏃 Easy Run30–40 min conversational pace. Active recovery.
WED
🏋️ Day BPull + Hinge dominant. Back, biceps, hamstrings, glutes.
THU
🏃 Tempo Run40 min. Comfortably hard. Threshold training.
FRI
🏋️ Day APush + Squat again. More volume on chest and quads this week.
SAT
🏃 Long Run50–70 min easy. Then swim 5:30–7:30pm.
SUN
⛪ Church3–6pm altar serve. Rest day from training.

📅 Week B Pattern

MON
🏋️ Day BPull + Hinge. Back, biceps, hamstrings, glutes.
TUE
🏃 Easy Run30–40 min conversational pace.
WED
🏋️ Day APush + Squat. Chest, shoulders, quads, triceps.
THU
🏃 Interval Run40 min. 6–8× 2 min hard / 2 min easy intervals.
FRI
🏋️ Day BPull + Hinge again. More back volume this week.
SAT
🏃 Long Run50–70 min easy. Then swim 5:30–7:30pm.
SUN
⛪ Church3–6pm altar serve. Rest day from training.
Why A/B instead of Push/Pull naming? Because it keeps continuing — A always follows B, B always follows A. The letter doesn't mean anything fixed. It's just your pattern. Simple, clean, and scalable for years.
G1 — Understanding Your Numbers

RPE, Tempo & Rest
Explained Simply

📊 RPE Scale (Rate of Perceived Exertion)

RPE measures how hard a set feels, from 1–10. You target RPE 7–8 for hypertrophy.

RPE 6Could do 4+ more reps. Too easy. Add weight next set.
RPE 7Could do 3 more reps. Good warm-up or early sets.
RPE 8Could do 2 more reps. This is your main work zone.
RPE 9Could do 1 more rep. Use on final set occasionally.
RPE 10True max effort. Never in regular training at 15.
Rule: If you hit the top of your rep range (e.g. 12 reps) at RPE 7 or below for 2 sessions in a row → increase weight by 1.25–2.5kg next session.

⏱ Tempo Notation (e.g. 3-1-2-0)

Tempo = how slowly you move each phase of the lift. Written as Eccentric – Pause – Concentric – Pause at top.

33 seconds lowering the weight (eccentric / stretch phase)
11 second pause at the bottom (full stretch)
22 seconds lifting the weight (concentric / power phase)
0No pause at top — go straight into next rep
Why tempo matters: Slowing the eccentric phase (lowering) is the most powerful driver of hypertrophy. Most beginners rush the lowering — this eliminates most of the muscle-building stimulus.

⏸ Rest Periods

60 secIsolation exercises (curls, lateral raises). Short rest keeps metabolic stress high.
75 secModerate compound accessories.
90 secMain compound lifts (squat, RDL, row, press). Standard rest for strength + hypertrophy.
2 minHeavy compound main lifts, last set at RPE 9. Max recovery.
Use a timer. Resting too long kills the hypertrophy stimulus. Resting too short means you can't maintain quality reps. 90 seconds is the default — use your phone timer every single set.
G2 — Perfect Day A

Day A: Push & Squat
Full Session

Session length: 75–90 minutes including warm-up and cool-down. Main focus: chest, shoulders, quads, triceps, core. Best for hypertrophy (muscle mass). Targets all pushing and squatting movements.

💡
Day A Goal: Compound pushing + quad dominance for maximum muscle growth in chest, shoulders, and legs. Start heavy, finish with accessories. Never skip warm-up.

🌡 Prehab Warm-Up (8 Minutes — Non-Negotiable)

ExerciseDuration/RepsPurpose
Hip circles (both directions)10 each directionHip flexor prep, prevents knee pain
Arm circles (front + back)15 each directionShoulder joint lubrication
Band pull-aparts20 repsRotator cuff activation — prevents shoulder injury
World's greatest stretch5 each side × 30sHip, thoracic, hamstring opening
Deep squat hold60 secondsAnkle + hip mobility for squat depth
Dead bugs6 each sideCore activation + lumbar stability
Bodyweight squat (2 sets)15 reps eachGroove squat pattern, warm joints
Push-up (warm-up set)10 repsShoulder + chest activation
Stop rules: If you feel sharp pain (not muscle burn) in any joint during warm-up — stop that movement and note which exercise caused it. See a GP or physio before training. Warm-up pain = your body sending a signal.
🛑
Red Flags — Stop Immediately: Sharp joint pain, numbness or tingling in arm/leg, sudden dizziness or nausea, sharp lower back pain, chest pain. Seek medical attention if these occur.
⚕️ Parent/Guardian Note: Ensure Kevin has completed a GP physical check before beginning. Growth plates at age 15 are still developing — all exercises below are selected for safety at this age.

Main Compound Lifts (Do in Order)

🏋️ A1 — Goblet Squat (Primary Leg Exercise)

4 SETS8–10 REPSTEMPO: 3-1-2-0REST: 90 SECRPE: 7–8

Hold one dumbbell vertically at chest. Feet shoulder-width, toes slightly out. Chest tall, core braced. Lower 3 seconds to full depth (thighs below parallel if mobility allows). Pause 1 second at bottom. Drive up 2 seconds through mid-foot.

  • Common fault: Heels rising off floor. Fix: elevate heels on a plate, or work ankle mobility daily.
  • Common fault: Chest collapsing forward. Fix: look slightly up, squeeze upper back, hold weight higher.
  • Progression: When 10 reps at RPE 7 for 2 sessions → increase weight 2.5kg.
  • Home alt: Bodyweight squat with resistance band for load, or backpack squat.

🏋️ A2 — Dumbbell Incline Press (Primary Chest)

4 SETS10–12 REPSTEMPO: 3-1-2-0REST: 90 SECRPE: 7–8

Bench at 30–45° incline. Dumbbells at shoulder level, elbows at 45°. Lower 3 seconds — feel full stretch in chest. Pause 1 second. Press up 2 seconds, don't fully lock elbows (keeps tension on muscle). Scapulae retracted and depressed throughout.

  • Common fault: Elbows flaring wide (90° from torso). Fix: bring to 45°. Protects shoulders.
  • Common fault: Arching back excessively. Fix: feet flat on floor, core engaged.
  • Why incline? Targets upper chest + front delt — the part of chest that creates a full look from the front. More valuable at your stage than flat press.
  • Home alt: Pike push-ups or feet-elevated push-ups on a chair.

🏋️ A3 — Dumbbell Shoulder Press (Primary Shoulder)

3 SETS10–12 REPSTEMPO: 2-1-2-0REST: 75 SECRPE: 7–8

Seated or standing. Dumbbells at ear height, elbows at 90°. Press overhead 2 seconds — don't shrug shoulders up. Slightly in front of ears (not directly overhead to protect shoulder impingement). Lower 2 seconds back to start. Keep core braced throughout.

  • Targets: All 3 deltoid heads, especially anterior and lateral — the "shoulder boulder" look.
  • Common fault: Shrugging traps on the press. Fix: actively depress shoulders down as you press up.
  • Home alt: Pike push-up or resistance band overhead press.

🏋️ A4 — Bulgarian Split Squat (Secondary Leg)

3 SETS8–10 EACH LEGTEMPO: 3-0-2-0REST: 90 SECRPE: 7–8

Rear foot elevated on bench (shoelaces down). Long stride forward. Lower 3 seconds — rear knee towards floor, front shin stays vertical. Push through front heel to rise. Hold dumbbells at sides or use bodyweight. Targets quads, glutes, and trains balance simultaneously.

  • Common fault: Front knee caving in. Fix: consciously drive knee out over pinky toe.
  • Why unilateral? Fixes left/right imbalances early. More glute activation than regular squat.
  • Home alt: Reverse lunge (no elevated foot). Same movement pattern.

Accessories & Isolation (Finish in Order)

A5 — Lateral Raise

3 SETS15–20 REPSREST: 60 SEC

Very light dumbbell (2–4kg). Arms slightly bent. Raise to shoulder height — pause 1 second at top. Lower slowly 3 seconds. Never go heavy on laterals. High rep, slow lowering = maximum lateral delt growth.

  • Lean very slightly forward (5–10°) — hits lateral delt better
  • Thumbs slightly down (like pouring water) — external rotation protects shoulder
  • Home alt: Resistance band attached to door frame

A6 — Tricep Dips

3 SETS10–15 REPSREST: 60 SEC

Use dip station or two sturdy chairs. Hands shoulder-width, elbows pointing back (not out). Lower slowly 3 seconds. Go to 90° elbow flexion. Press up fully. Keep torso upright — leaning forward shifts to chest.

  • Progression: Add weight on lap when bodyweight becomes easy
  • Stop if shoulder pain at bottom position — reduce depth first
  • Home alt: Chair dips (hands behind you on chair seat)

A7 — Calf Raise

4 SETS20–25 REPSREST: 45 SEC

Stand on edge of step with heels hanging off. Rise onto tiptoes — pause 1 second at top. Lower fully below step level — pause 1 second at bottom. Full range of motion. Calves need very high volume — they're worked all day walking.

  • Hold dumbbells for added load
  • Vary foot angle: straight = gastrocnemius, toes out = inner head
  • Home alt: Off any step or stair

🎯 Core Finisher (End of Every Day A — 8 Min)

Dead Bug — 3 × 8 each side

Lie on back, arms up, knees 90°. Extend opposite arm + leg while lower back presses into floor. Return. Slow and controlled. Never let lower back arch off floor.

Side Plank — 3 × 30–45 seconds each

Elbow on floor, body in straight line. Stack feet or stagger them. Hips square and high. This targets obliques + prevents spinal rotation under load.

Hollow Hold — 3 × 20–30 seconds

Lie on back, arms by ears, legs extended. Lift both off ground maintaining low back flat. Creates the exact body position needed for good posture and bracing under load.

❄ Cool-Down (10 Min — Do Every Session)

  • Hip flexor lunge stretch × 60s each side (essential after squats)
  • Quad stretch standing × 45s each
  • Pigeon pose glute stretch × 90s each side
  • Chest opener (arms behind back, squeeze blades) × 60s
  • Overhead tricep stretch × 45s each side
  • Shoulder cross-body stretch × 45s each
  • Cat-camel spinal mobilisation × 10 reps
  • Deep breathing — 5 slow breaths, feel heart rate drop

Total session time: 75–90 minutes. Don't rush. Quality reps build more muscle than rushed reps. If tired, reduce weight — never reduce technique.
G3 — Perfect Day B

Day B: Pull & Hinge
Full Session

Session length: 75–90 minutes. Main focus: back, biceps, hamstrings, glutes, rear delts. The pulling and hinging muscles are your biggest posture fixers and back builders. Most beginners neglect these — don't.

💡
Day B Goal: Build a wide, thick back + powerful posterior chain. Hamstrings and glutes are your athletic power engines. This day makes you look good from behind AND makes you faster and stronger in every sport.

🌡 Prehab Warm-Up — Day B (8 Minutes)

ExerciseDuration/RepsPurpose
Cat-camel spinal mobilisation10 repsSpine prep before hinging movements
Hip hinge practice (no weight)15 repsGroove the RDL pattern — essential before loading
Band pull-aparts25 repsRear delt + rotator cuff activation
Side-lying clamshell20 each sideGlute med activation — prevents knee issues
Leg swings (front-back + side-side)15 each direction each legHip flexor + hamstring prep
Dead bug6 each sideCore activation before RDL
Inverted row (warm-up set)10 repsActivate lats before main pulls

Main Compound Lifts (Do in Order)

🏋️ B1 — Romanian Deadlift (Primary Hamstring/Glute)

4 SETS8–10 REPSTEMPO: 3-1-1-0REST: 90 SECRPE: 7–8

Dumbbells in front of thighs. Push hips BACK (not knees down). Lower 3 seconds — keep the bar close to legs, back flat, slight knee bend. Stop when you feel a strong hamstring stretch (usually mid-shin level). Pause 1 second. Drive hips forward 1 second to return. This is a HIP HINGE not a squat.

  • Common fault: Rounding the lower back. Fix: think "chest up, push hips back" — not "bend over."
  • Common fault: Going too deep past hamstring flexibility. Fix: stop at your maximum hamstring stretch, not the floor.
  • This is your most important exercise for athletic development. Hamstrings are the most commonly injured muscle in all running sports.
  • Home alt: Single-leg RDL with no weight — balance challenge makes it hard enough.

🏋️ B2 — Pull-Ups or Inverted Rows (Primary Back)

4 SETSMAX REPSTEMPO: 2-1-3-0REST: 90 SECRPE: 8

Pull-ups (if you can do 3+): Dead hang grip (thumbs around bar), pull until chin over bar, lower 3 seconds slowly. No swinging, no kipping. If can't do pull-ups yet → Inverted rows: bar at hip height, body straight, pull chest to bar, lower 3 seconds. Both hit lats hard.

  • Progression for pull-ups: If doing 5+, add a backpack with weight. If doing less than 3, do negatives (jump up, lower 5 seconds).
  • Common fault: Shrugging traps at the top. Fix: think "pull elbows to back pockets."
  • Why max reps? Pull-up volume is your primary back width builder. Every rep counts.
  • Target over 12 weeks: From baseline to 10+ strict pull-ups.

🏋️ B3 — Dumbbell Row (Secondary Back)

3 SETS12–15 EACHTEMPO: 2-1-3-0REST: 75 SECRPE: 7–8

One knee and hand on bench, flat back. Dumbbell hanging from opposite arm. Row elbow to hip — not to shoulder. Think "elbow to back pocket." Lower 3 seconds. Scapula should retract fully at top, then fully protract at bottom. Don't rotate torso.

  • Common fault: Rowing to shoulder (hits trap, not lat). Fix: row elbow to hip.
  • Common fault: Using bicep too much. Fix: think "lead with the elbow, not the hand."
  • Home alt: Resistance band row attached to door frame at waist height.

🏋️ B4 — Face Pull / Rear Delt Fly (Rear Shoulder)

3 SETS20 REPSTEMPO: 2-1-2-0REST: 60 SECRPE: 7

Face pull: Band attached at face height, grip with thumbs up, pull to face while pulling elbows back and out. OR Rear delt fly: Bent over 45°, arms hanging. Raise arms out like wings — pause at top. Lower slowly. Very light weight only.

  • Why so important: Your rear delts and rotator cuff are the most underworked muscles for people who do pressing. Neglecting them causes shoulder injury. This exercise is injury prevention.
  • Home alt: Resistance band face pull attached to door at head height.

Accessories & Isolation

B5 — Bicep Curl

3 SETS12–15 REPSREST: 60 SEC

Alternating dumbbell curl. Supinate wrist fully at top (palm up). Lower 3 seconds. Don't swing elbows forward. Your biceps already get significant work from rows and pull-ups — these curls are finishing volume.

  • Supination (wrist rotation) = more bicep peak
  • Keep elbows pinned to sides — don't let them drift forward
  • Home alt: Resistance band bicep curl

B6 — Single-Leg Glute Bridge

3 SETS15 EACHREST: 60 SEC

Lie on back, one foot flat on floor, other leg extended. Drive hips up through the grounded heel until body is straight line. Pause 1 second at top squeezing glute. Lower slowly. Your glute max is your most powerful running and jumping muscle.

  • Progress: add dumbbell on hip, or elevate foot on bench
  • Squeeze glute HARD at top — not just "lift up"

B7 — Nordic Curl (Hamstring)

3 SETS4–6 REPSREST: 90 SEC

Kneel with feet anchored under furniture. Cross arms over chest. Lower your body SLOWLY (3–5 seconds) — control the fall as long as possible with hamstrings. When you can't hold, catch yourself on hands and push back up. The most powerful hamstring injury prevention exercise.

  • The eccentric (lowering) is the exercise — the push back up doesn't matter
  • Research shows this reduces hamstring injury rate by up to 50%
  • Start with partial range (partial fall) — build to full over weeks

🎯 Core Finisher — Day B (8 Min)

Bird Dog — 3 × 10 each side

On hands and knees. Extend opposite arm and leg simultaneously. Hold 2 seconds, return without touching floor. Spine stays completely neutral — imagine balancing a water glass on your lower back.

Pallof Press — 3 × 12 each side

Band anchored at chest height. Stand side-on. Hold band at chest, press straight out in front — resist rotation. Core must fight the rotational pull. Trains anti-rotation stability.

Plank — 3 × 45–60 seconds

Forearms on floor, body completely straight. No hips sagging or piking. Squeeze every muscle — quads, glutes, abs, lats. Think "protect your spine from being bent."

G4 — 12-Week Mesocycle

Week-by-Week
Progression Plan

3 weeks of progressive build followed by 1 deload week. Repeat 3 times = 12 weeks total. Each block increases volume or intensity slightly. This is the mechanism by which muscle grows.

📈 Progression Rules

  • Rep target hit at RPE ≤7 for 2 consecutive sessions → Increase weight by 1.25–2.5kg next session. This is the only signal to add weight.
  • Rep target NOT hit → Stay at same weight. Fix form first, then attempt to hit reps.
  • RPE 9+ on working sets regularly → Weight is too heavy. Drop 5% and rebuild.
  • Deload week (every 4th week) → Drop all weights by 30–40%, reduce sets to 2 per exercise. This is when muscle grows. Do not skip deloads.
  • Weigh-in every Monday morning. Post-toilet, pre-food, same clothes. Target +0.3–0.5kg per week.

🗓 Mesocycle Overview

WeekPhaseSets (Main Lifts)Focus
Week 1Base3 sets × 12 repsLearn form, light weight
Week 2Build4 sets × 10 repsAdd weight if ready
Week 3Overload4 sets × 8 repsHeaviest week, RPE 8
Week 4DELOAD2 sets × 12 reps50–60% weight, recovery
Week 5Base 23 sets × 12 repsHeavier than Week 1
Week 6Build 24 sets × 10 repsProgressive overload
Week 7Overload 25 sets × 8 repsExtra volume added
Week 8DELOAD2 sets × 12 repsRecovery and adaptation
Week 9Base 34 sets × 12 repsHeavier again
Week 10Build 34–5 sets × 10Peak volume block
Week 11Overload 35 sets × 6–8Heaviest block yet
Week 12TEST WEEK3RM test main liftsMeasure progress
G5 — Clean Bulk Guide (Nut-Free)

Getting Bigger: The
Nut-Free Bulk System

⚠️
KEVIN'S NUT ALLERGY — ALWAYS CHECK LABELS. No peanuts, no tree nuts (almonds, cashews, walnuts, pecans, macadamias, pistachios). Always carry your EpiPen. If eating at restaurants or buying packaged food — ask about cross-contamination. Every recipe and food in this section is nut-free.

📊 Kevin's Daily Calorie Targets

At 50kg / 175cm training 6× per week, your maintenance is ~2,400–2,600 kcal. To gain 0.3–0.5kg/week (clean lean bulk), you need:

Daily Calories3,200–3,500 kcal
Protein140–160g/day (2.8–3.2g/kg)
Carbohydrates400–450g/day
Fats90–110g/day
Track for 2 weeks in MyFitnessPal. Most skinny teenagers eat 1,400–1,800 kcal thinking they're eating a lot. Data kills excuses.

💡 8 Appetite Hacks for Hard Gainers (From Real Experience)

From the iLoveMerge transcript — someone who went from 55kg to 90kg: "The biggest mistake was not eating on a timer. I waited until I was hungry. But skinny guys who undereat have suppressed ghrelin (hunger hormone). You have to force-feed yourself for 3–4 weeks until appetite catches up. Then it becomes automatic."

  • Eat on a timer every 2.5–3 hours. Set phone alarms. Ignore hunger signals for the first 4 weeks — they're lying to you.
  • Liquid calories first. Chocolate milk 500ml = 330 kcal in 2 minutes. If you can't eat solids, drink whole milk, smoothies, chocolate milk constantly.
  • Add olive oil to everything savoury. 1 tablespoon = 120 kcal. Barely tastes of anything. Every meal gets a drizzle.
  • Calorie dense, low volume. Avocado, whole milk, olive oil, full-fat Greek yoghurt. Max calories, minimum stomach volume.
  • Walk 10 minutes after every meal. Accelerates gastric emptying — you'll be hungry again 40% faster.
  • 6 meals × 550 kcal feels infinitely easier than 3 × 1,100 kcal. Never try to eat massive meals.
  • Track in MyFitnessPal for 14 days. You will be shocked how much less than you think you're eating. Data changes everything.
  • Two Minute Rice (400 kcal). Microwave pouch. Fast, cheap, easy calorie source for between meals or when appetite is low.

✅ Supplements — What Kevin Actually Needs

  • Vitamin D3 (1,000–2,000 IU daily): Even in Sydney many teens are deficient. Critical for bone health and muscle function. Take with fatty meal.
  • Zinc (15mg daily): Suppressed appetite and low testosterone are symptoms of zinc deficiency — extremely common in athletes. 4-week trial.
  • Magnesium glycinate (200–300mg before bed): Improves deep sleep quality, reduces muscle cramps, aids recovery. Very effective and safe.
  • Creatine monohydrate (3g daily) — AFTER AGE 16 WITH GP APPROVAL: Most researched sports supplement. Increases strength and muscle by 5–10%. Safe, effective, cheap.

❌ Supplements to Skip

  • Pre-workout: High caffeine disrupts sleep at 15. You have the highest natural testosterone of your life — you don't need stimulants.
  • BCAAs: Redundant with adequate protein intake. Marketing product.
  • Expensive "mass gainer" shakes: Often full of sugar and cheap maltodextrin. Make your own smoothie — it's better and cheaper.
  • Fat burners / anything with a "shred" label: Completely counterproductive during a bulk. Never.
⚕️ Parent Note: All supplements should be GP-approved before use. Growth plates are still developing at 15. Food should always come first. Any supplement taken must be checked for nut cross-contamination.

Kevin's Weekly Meal Prep Strategy

📅 Sunday Prep (30 minutes) — Sets Up Your Entire Week

Parents cook dinner, but you can prep these items yourself:

  • Hard-boil 12 eggs: Boil 10 min, cool, refrigerate. Perfect for quick breakfasts or snacks.
  • Cook 2 cups of rice: Use rice cooker or pot. Refrigerate in container. Use for meals all week.
  • Wash and prep vegetables: Cut broccoli, carrots, bell peppers. Store in containers.
  • Protein shake bags: Pre-measure whey protein + oats into ziplock bags. Morning: add milk + blend.
  • Chicken breast batch: Cook 500g chicken breast. Slice. Refrigerate. Add to meals all week.
  • Greek yoghurt portions: Divide 1kg tub into 5 containers. Add honey/granola when eating.
  • Tuna cans: Stock 6 cans. Keep in pantry. Emergency protein when you're behind.
  • Cheese slices: Pre-slice cheddar. Keep in container. Add to eggs, toast, sandwiches.
  • Bananas: Buy 15. Eat 2-3 daily. Perfect pre-workout carb.

G6 — The Science Made Simple

How Muscle Growth
Actually Works

🔬 The 3 Mechanisms of Hypertrophy

  • Mechanical Tension: When a muscle is under load and stretch simultaneously — this is why the LOWERING (eccentric) phase is most important. Slow eccentrics = more tension time = more growth signal.
  • Metabolic Stress: The "pump" — blood pooling + lactate in the muscle. Shorter rest periods, higher rep ranges. Contributes to hypertrophy through cell swelling and hormonal response.
  • Muscle Damage: Microscopic tears from exercise — repaired bigger during sleep. Not the primary driver, but contributes. Why you're sore 24–48h after training.
Translation: For maximum muscle growth, slow down the eccentric phase, use moderate weights with controlled form, and sleep 8–9 hours every night. There is no shortcut to this.

📊 Minimum Effective Dose Per Muscle Group

Research shows 4–6 sets per muscle group per week is enough to gain muscle. Kevin's A/B plan targets:

MuscleSets/Week (A/B/A week)Target
Chest8 sets (2×Day A incline)✓ Above minimum
Shoulders9 sets (2×press + 2×laterals)✓ Above minimum
Triceps7 sets (from pressing + dips)✓ Above minimum
Back (lats)7 sets (1×Day B pull/row)✓ Above minimum
Biceps7 sets (from rows + curls)✓ Above minimum
Quads8 sets (2×Day A squat/BSS)✓ Above minimum
Hamstrings5 sets (1×Day B RDL/nordic)✓ Above minimum
Glutes6 sets (from RDL + bridge)✓ Above minimum
Calves8 sets (2×Day A calf raise)✓ Above minimum

In an A/B/A week, push muscles get 2× volume. In B/A/B week, pull muscles get 2× volume. Perfectly balanced across the fortnight.

😴 Recovery: The Actual Work

  • Muscle grows during SLEEP, not during training. Training is the stimulus. Sleep is when protein synthesis happens. No sleep = no gains.
  • Target 8–9 hours every night. At 15, you need more sleep than adults due to active hormonal development. Growth hormone peaks in first 2–3 hours of deep sleep.
  • Protein within 60 min post-training. Muscle protein synthesis is elevated for 24–48h but highest in first hour after training. Hit 30–40g protein within 30–60 min of gym.
  • 48–72 hours between sessions for same muscle group. Day A on Monday → can do Day A again Wednesday or Thursday. Your A/B pattern already ensures this automatically.
G7 — From Real People Who Did It

Lessons from Skinny Guys
Who Actually Bulked Up

These insights are distilled from multiple video transcripts of real people who went from skinny to jacked — people who started where you are. Every tip here has been validated by someone who lived it, plus research. Personalised for Kevin's exact situation.

✦
Kevin's situation matches perfectly: 50kg, ectomorph, fast metabolism, home gym, 15 years old with peak testosterone and neuroplasticity. Multiple people who made 30+ kg transformations started exactly where you are. The ones who succeeded all had the same thing in common: they were consistent — not perfect.

🧠 Lesson 1: Fix Your Mental State First

From someone who went from 60–62kg to jacked: "The main cause of me not progressing was mindlessly scrolling. It gave me temporary relief but moved me nowhere near my goals."

  • Social media kills your bulk progress indirectly — it lowers motivation, disrupts sleep, and creates constant comparison that breeds inaction.
  • Action: Set screen time limits (iPhone Settings → Screen Time → App Limits). Target: under 30 min/day on entertainment apps.
  • Replace scrolling with: Training, music, walks, genuine friends. The dopamine from real progress is infinitely more powerful than a feed.
  • When you reduce social media, you'll feel temporarily worse for 3–7 days. This is dopamine recalibration — it's normal and it passes.
Kevin-specific: Your phone in the kitchen at 10pm (already in your timetable) is already addressing this. The morning commute — podcast only, no scrolling — compounds this further.

⚡ Lesson 2: Consistency > Optimization

From research on natural bodybuilders: The biggest predictor of how jacked someone becomes isn't their split, their rep scheme, or their supplements. It's whether they trained consistently for years and never stopped.

  • The person who trains 2×/week and never misses will beat the person who trains 6×/week then takes a month off every time — every single time.
  • Your A/B/A → B/A/B system solves this. 3 sessions/week is enough. The plan is sustainable. That's the entire design.
  • Never miss 2 sessions in a row. Missing one is fine. Missing two starts breaking the habit loop. One missed session → immediate rescheduling.
  • Even on bad days: Go to your gym space, do the warm-up, do at least 2 sets of each main lift. "Something" beats nothing. Progress is always forward, never backward.

🏋️ Lesson 3: Compound Lifts First, Always

From skinny guy who went from 55kg to 90kg: "Always do compound lifts at the start of your session when you have the most energy, strength, and power."

  • If you start with bicep curls then try rows — your fatigued arms can't support the back work. You waste the most important exercise of the session.
  • Order in your sessions: 1) Main compound (squat/RDL/press/pull-up) → 2) Secondary compound → 3) Isolation/accessories. This is already built into your Day A and Day B.
  • Best compound lifts for Kevin: Incline dumbbell press (chest), pull-ups (back), goblet squat (quads), Romanian deadlift (hamstrings), shoulder press (shoulders). These 5 exercises alone will build the majority of your muscle.
  • Upper body structure per day: 2 exercises for chest, 2 for back, 1–2 for shoulders, 1–2 for arms. Max 7–8 exercises per session.

🍹 Lesson 4: Liquid Calories Are the Hard Gainer's Cheat Code

Mentioned in almost every single video from someone who successfully bulked from skinny: "Drinking 1,000 calories is infinitely easier than eating 1,000 calories."

The "Phaser Shake" — ~1,000 kcal, high protein, nut-free version for Kevin:

IngredientAmountKcalProteinNotes
Whole milk400ml26214gNon-negotiable base
Frozen banana (ripe)2 medium1902gNatural sweetener + carbs
Rolled oats80g2908gSlow-release energy
Whey protein (nut-free check)1.5 scoops 45g18037gCheck label for nut-free certification
Honey1.5 tbsp96—Palatability + quick carbs
Sunflower seed butter (nut-free)1 tbsp953gHealthy fats — nut-free alternative
TOTAL~1,11364gBlend 60 seconds
  • Prep the night before: Put everything except milk in a bag. Morning: pour milk in blender + bag contents. Blend 60 sec. Done in under 3 minutes.
  • When to drink it: Post-gym within 30 min, OR as breakfast if you can't eat solids in the morning, OR as a top-up when you're 800–1,000 kcal short of your daily target.
  • ⚠️ NUT ALLERGY: Always verify your whey protein powder has a "nut-free facility" certification on the label. EpiPen accessible at all times.

⏱ Lesson 5: The 10-Minute No-Distraction Timer Method

From Joe's bulking guide: "When you're distracted eating, your brain has time to realize it's full — so you eat less. Cut distractions. Set a timer. Focus only on eating for those minutes."

  • Set a 10-minute timer for every meal. Put your phone face-down (or in another room). No TV. No YouTube. Pure eating focus. Your stomach physically cannot send "full" signals to your brain fast enough during 10 focused minutes — you'll finish larger portions.
  • Drink 500ml water 20–30 min before meals (not during or right after). Water expands your stomach capacity. Multiple videos confirmed this works. Pre-meal water = more food fits in.
  • Eat at the same times every day — set phone alarms. From the habit research: when gym time hits, you go automatically. Same with meals — when the alarm goes off, you eat, even if you're not hungry. After 3–4 weeks your hunger hormones recalibrate to this schedule.
  • Track everything in MyFitnessPal for 14 days. Multiple people were shocked to find they were eating 1,400–1,800 kcal when they thought they were eating loads. Data eliminates self-deception.

📈 Lesson 6: Progressive Overload — Track Every Session

From someone who went from 55kg to 90kg: "I monitored my lifts on my Notes app. Every time I did a lift I wrote it down and could see the progress I was making over time."

  • Open Notes app (or download Boostcamp/Strong). After every set, log: exercise, weight, reps. Example: "Goblet Squat — 20kg × 10 reps, RPE 8."
  • The only goal each session: Beat last session by at least 1 rep or 1.25kg. Progress doesn't have to be dramatic — it just has to always be going forward.
  • Progressive overload is THE mechanism of muscle growth. Without it you're exercising, not training. With it, you're compounding results every single week.
  • At 15 with high testosterone, you'll hit personal records faster than any adult starting from scratch. Your strength will jump noticeably week-to-week in the first 3–4 months.

😴 Lesson 7: Sleep is the Actual Supplement

From Dr. Andrew Spectre (Duke University, sleep specialist): "If sleep weren't a normal thing, it would be a banned substance. The body produces more growth hormone and testosterone when you slept better."

  • Growth hormone and testosterone peak during deep sleep (roughly 10pm–2am). Kevin's 10pm bedtime is scientifically optimal for his age.
  • 3 sleep quality hacks from the research: (1) Room completely dark — cover all LED lights, use eye mask. (2) Cool temperature — open window or fan, aim for 18°C. (3) Quiet — earplugs if needed.
  • Even 2 nights of bad sleep measurably reduces testosterone and growth hormone release. This is why "one bad night" visibly affects your gym performance and how your face looks.
  • Naps work: If you slept badly, a 20-minute nap before training restores athletic performance almost fully.

🔁 Lesson 8: Habit Stacking for Automatic Gains

From Brett's bulking guide: "Temptation bundling — I drink my bulk shake after the gym while watching fitness YouTube. My annoyance at being skinny is still fresh. Pairing it with an enjoyable activity meant I actually stuck to it."

  • Attach new habits to existing ones. You already study → add a 400-kcal snack before studying. You already shower → add skincare routine after. You already wake up → add 600ml water immediately.
  • 2-minute trigger: When it's gym time, put on gym clothes and play your training playlist. At this point it becomes more uncomfortable NOT to train.
  • Build a stash: Greek yoghurt, oats, milk, rice cakes, sunflower seeds always in the kitchen. When the snack alarm goes off, food is immediately available — zero friction.
  • The goal: Make bulking as easy as possible. Not willpower — systems. Your timetable, your stash, your alarms are your systems.

🔬 Lesson 9: Science of Sets — The Minimum Effective Dose

From Dr. Mike Israetel's research (Florida Atlantic University): "4 sets per muscle group per week is the minimum effective dose. Natural bodybuilders averaging top-level physiques only do ~12 sets per muscle per week — probably less than what you're planning."

Sets 1–5 per muscle/week:

Maximum growth stimulus. Every set gives significant return.

Sets 5–10 per muscle/week:

Good returns. Still growing fast. Where Kevin's plan operates.

Sets 10–20 per muscle/week:

Diminishing returns. More time, less extra growth.

Sets 20+/week:

Potential overtraining. Recovery suffers. Gains may decrease.

Kevin's plan hits 5–10 sets/muscle/week across the fortnight. This is the optimal zone — maximum growth for minimum recovery cost. Perfect for an athlete who also runs and swims 4× per week.

💡 Lesson 10: Training to Failure — The Right Way

From Jeremy Ethier's research with Dr. Mike Israetel: "When you stop 8 reps short of failure, muscles still grow. When you push to 1–2 reps short of failure, growth nearly doubles."

  • RPE 8 (2 reps in reserve) = the optimal zone for Kevin. Not going to true failure every set (which causes excessive soreness and recovery issues), but not coasting either. RPE 8 means you're leaving exactly 2 reps in the tank.
  • How to know you're training hard enough: (1) Last rep should move noticeably slower than first rep. (2) On your last set, you shouldn't be able to do significantly more reps than your first set. If you can — you're going too easy.
  • The mental challenge: RPE 8 is uncomfortable. That discomfort is the signal you're in the growth zone. Lean into it — it ends when the set ends.
  • Frequency beats failure: Rather than burning out one muscle completely on one day, spread sets across 2 days/week (your A/B pattern already does this). 30% more growth from this alone.

Two weeks. No school. Maximum transformation. This plan covers body, skin, hair, style, mindset, habits, fitness, and nutrition — designed to return you to school unrecognisably better in every visible and invisible way.

14-Day Plan Body Skin Style Mindset Habits
The Foundation

Why Holidays Are
The Perfect Transformation Window

The Compound Effect of 14 Days

Two weeks seems short. But 14 days of optimal sleep, clean eating, consistent training, proper skincare, and no school stress creates visible, measurable changes that last. The holidays remove the biggest obstacle: irregular schedule. You own your time completely.

  • Sleep: 8–9 hours every night compounded = 14 nights of maximum growth hormone. Your face, body, and energy will look different by day 10.
  • Training: 10 gym sessions + 6 runs in 14 days = more volume than a typical 4-week school term.
  • Skin: A new skincare routine shows visible improvement in 10–14 days if you're consistent every single day.
  • Posture: Daily corrective work for 14 days creates measurable postural change that carries back to school.
  • Confidence: The combination of improved appearance + physical achievements + no social pressure = massive internal shift.

The Glow-Up Rules

  • Phone budget: 1 hour/day max. The holidays are when the phone addiction accelerates worst. The transformation and the phone use cannot coexist. Pick one.
  • Sleep is non-negotiable. Every late night resets all the hormonal and skin work you did that day. 22:00 lights out. 06:30 rise.
  • Document the process. Take a front + side photo on Day 1, Day 7, and Day 14. Progress you cannot see day-to-day becomes obvious week to week.
  • One focus area per day. Don't try to fix everything at once. Each day has a theme. Execute that theme completely before worrying about the next.
  • No zero days. Something every day. Even a 20-minute walk and 5-minute skincare routine beats a complete rest day where nothing was done intentionally.

Compound the Gains

Days 8–10 — Load Week

DAY 8 — Push (heavier than Week 1)

DAY 9 — Legs + Confidence Mission
Heavier legs session. Confidence mission: go to a coffee shop, order a drink, sit without your phone for 30 minutes. Observe the room. This is an exercise in presence.
DAY 10 — Pull (heavier) + Grooming Deep Clean
Heavier pull session + weighted pull-ups if ready. Full grooming audit: nails, eyebrows (barber clean-up), lip balm, nose/ear hair check, deodorant routine. Smell good → feel good.

Days 11–14 — Peak Week

DAY 11 — Arms/Core + Voice Training
Voice training: record yourself speaking for 2 min. Count filler words (um, like, you know). Practise speaking 20% slower than natural. Read a passage aloud, focusing on diaphragm breathing. Arms + core session PM.
DAY 12 — Full Body (heaviest day)
Full body compound session — deadlifts, bench, rows, weighted pull-ups. Beat every number from Week 1. This is a test day. Prove Week 2 progress.
DAY 13 — Long Run (longer than Week 1)
Long run: 5–10 min longer than Day 6. New route. After: meal prep for first week back at school. Lay out outfits for school. Plan Week 1 back schedule.
DAY 14 — Final Day Review + Reset
Day 14 final photos (front + side). Compare Day 1 vs Day 14. Write: what changed physically? What changed internally? What habits continue into term? Pack school bag with intention. Sleep early.
✨ Deep Dive Guides

Every Glow-Up Category
In Depth

🧴 14-Day Skin Protocol

Visible results in 10–14 days if consistent. Zero misses allowed.

  • AM: CeraVe Foaming Cleanser (cool water, 30s) → CeraVe AM SPF 30 (apply to slightly damp skin). Always. Sun is the enemy of good skin.
  • PM: Same cleanser → CeraVe PM Moisturiser. On Days 3, 6, 9, 12 add The Ordinary 2% BHA Salicylic to any breakout areas only.
  • Body: Shower daily (post-training at minimum). Moisturise entire body immediately after shower while damp. Lips: CeraVe Healing Ointment nightly.
  • Diet hacks for skin: Cut sugar by 70% for 14 days. Drink 3L water. Eat salmon or sardines 3× this week (omega-3s reduce inflammation dramatically). You'll see results.
  • Change pillowcase every 3 days. Unwashed pillowcases carry accumulated oil and bacteria for 8 hours every night against your face.
  • Don't touch your face. Your hands have bacteria. Every unconscious face touch is a potential breakout.

💈 Hair Transformation Protocol

  • Day 4: Haircut. This is the biggest single-day visual change possible. Show your barber a reference photo. Ask for a style you can replicate at home with minimal product.
  • Wash schedule: Every 2 days (not daily). Overwashing = overproduction of oil = greasier hair. The sweet spot is alternate days.
  • Product technique: Take a 5-cent piece of clay or matte wax. Rub between palms until it disappears. Work through slightly damp hair (not soaking wet). Style with fingers or a wide-tooth comb. Never use gel — looks dated and damages hair.
  • Cold rinse: Last 10 seconds of every shower, rinse your hair with cool water. Closes the cuticle, adds shine, reduces frizz.
  • Growth: Take the zinc supplement daily. Zinc deficiency is the most common cause of slow hair growth and thinning in teenage boys. Give it 30 days.

🦴 Posture in 14 Days

  • The problem: Screen time creates forward head posture (chin forward), rounded shoulders, and anterior pelvic tilt. This makes you look shorter, weaker, and less confident — and it's completely fixable.
  • Daily corrective circuit (12 min): Wall angels × 15 → chin tucks × 20 → thoracic foam roll 2 min → hip flexor stretch 60s each → cat-camel × 10. Done every single morning.
  • The phone fix: Raise your phone to eye level when using it. "Tech neck" adds 2–3kg of force on your cervical spine per centimetre of forward tilt.
  • The sitting fix: Set an alarm every 90 minutes during holidays. When it goes off — stand, do 5 chin tucks, reset posture, sit back down. This alone creates change in 2 weeks.
  • The cue: "Tall spine, chest proud, shoulders back and down." Say this in your head every time you enter a room, every time you catch your reflection, every time you sit down.
  • By Day 14: Other people will notice. You will carry yourself differently and photographs will look dramatically better.

👕 Style in 14 Days

  • Wardrobe audit Day 1: Pull everything out. Three piles: keep, donate, bin. If you haven't worn it in 3 months or it doesn't fit — it goes. Less is more.
  • The 3 pieces that change everything: (1) One well-fitted white or black t-shirt, (2) well-fitted dark jeans or chinos, (3) clean white sneakers. These three items together are 80% of what looking good requires.
  • Fit rule: At 175cm/50kg, slim-fit is your friend. Clothes should skim your body, not hang off it or be skin-tight. Try items on — don't buy from photos.
  • Colour palette: Black, white, grey, navy, olive. These all go together. Avoid patterns until you master basics. One outfit should be wearable from a single rack of these colours.
  • Shoes matter most: One pair of clean, white low-top sneakers (AF1, New Balance 550, Adidas Campus) elevates every casual outfit instantly. Clean them weekly.
  • The signature item: One consistent accessory. A specific cap, a simple chain, a watch. Not multiple — one. Something distinctly yours that you always wear.

🎙 Voice & Presence

  • The voice dial: Record yourself speaking for 2 minutes on Day 1. Count filler words (um, like, you know, sort of). Most people have 15–30 per minute. Zero is the goal. Silence beats filler.
  • Slow down: Speak 20% slower than feels natural. Fast talking signals anxiety. Deliberate, measured speech signals confidence and authority — at any age.
  • Diaphragm breathing for voice: Shallow chest breathing produces a thin, weak voice. Belly breathing deepens resonance. Practice: place hand on belly, breathe in (belly rises), speak from that breath.
  • Eye contact rule: 70% while listening, 50% while speaking. More is intimidating, less is evasive. Practice with family and friends this week — no looking away when they speak.
  • The pause: When asked a question, pause 1–2 seconds before answering. This seems strange at first. It reads as confident and thoughtful to everyone else.
  • Physical presence: Feet shoulder-width when standing. Don't cross arms. Don't put hands in pockets nervously. Expand, don't contract.

🧠 Mindset Reset in 14 Days

  • The dopamine fast: For the first 3 days of holidays, no social media at all. Not even "just checking." Your brain needs to recalibrate. By Day 4, everything will feel noticeably clearer.
  • Morning intention (60 seconds every day): Before any screen, write one sentence: "Today I will [specific action] because I want to become [specific identity]." This takes 60 seconds and sets the neurological frame for the day.
  • Daily hard thing: Choose one thing each day that is uncomfortable and do it before noon. Cold shower, difficult call, hardest exercise, asking for something you might be rejected for. This builds the mental toughness muscle.
  • Evening reflection (3 min): Write 3 wins (no matter how small), 1 thing to improve tomorrow, 1 gratitude. Do this with a pen in a notebook — not on your phone.
  • The identity statement: Write your identity statement on a card. Read it every morning before leaving the bathroom. By Day 14, you will have started to believe it.
  • Social comparison detox: For 14 days, zero comparison to other people on social media. What others look like or do is completely irrelevant to your development. Focus inward only.

🏋️ Your Home Gym

You Have a Real Gym
at Home — Use It Right.

Most people pay $80/month for less than what you already have. Here's exactly how to use every piece of equipment you own to maximise muscle growth.

🔝 Power Tower / Pull-Up Bar / Chin-Up Station

This is your single most valuable piece of equipment. The pull-up is the greatest back exercise on the planet — better than most gym lat pulldown machines.

  • Wide-grip pull-up: Lat width — the V-taper look. Elbows out, pull chest to bar.
  • Neutral-grip chin-up: Lats + peak bicep. Best arm builder you have.
  • Close-grip chin-up: More bicep focus. Full range of motion.
  • Archer pull-up: One arm pulls, other arm guides — builds toward one-arm pull-up. Insane strength builder.
  • L-sit pull-up: Legs straight out parallel to floor while pulling. Core + back together.
  • Hanging leg raise: Best lower ab exercise. Legs straight, raise to 90° or higher.
  • Dead hang: 30–60s holds for grip strength and shoulder decompression.
  • Scapula pull-up: Warm-up movement. Depress scapula before adding pull-up reps.
Progression: Dead hang → Scapula pulls → Jumping pull-ups with slow negative → Full pull-ups → Weighted pull-ups with backpack or dip belt.

💪 Dip Station

Dips are the "upper body squat." Absolutely brutal for chest, triceps, and anterior shoulders when done correctly.

  • Chest dip (lean forward 30°): Lower chest and inner chest thickness. The best bodyweight chest exercise.
  • Tricep dip (body upright): Full tricep isolation. Locks out at the top. Go slow on the negative.
  • Weighted dip (backpack): Once bodyweight dips are easy (15+ reps), load up your backpack with books or water. Add weight every week.
  • Korean dip: Shoulders behind the bar, then press out. Advanced. Incredible shoulder/chest combo.
  • Negative dip: Jump to top position, lower yourself in 5 seconds. Use if full dips are too hard yet.
  • Ring dip (if you get rings): Unstable surface makes this 3× harder. Elite chest development.
Form: Elbows at 45° from your body — never fully flared. Chest up. Full depth (arms at 90° or below). Control the negative.

🏋️ Weight Bench + Barbell + Weights

Having a barbell and bench at home is elite. You can now do the same big compound lifts as any gym.

  • Barbell bench press: King of chest exercises. Grip slightly wider than shoulder-width. Touch chest, full lockout. Start light, master form first.
  • Incline dumbbell/barbell press: Bench at 30–45°. Upper chest and front delts. Critical for that "full chest" look from the top.
  • Barbell row (bent over): Best back thickness exercise. Hinge at hips, pull bar to lower chest/navel. Overhand or underhand grip.
  • Barbell curl: Classic bicep mass builder. Can go heavier than dumbbell curls.
  • Skull crusher (EZ bar / barbell): Lying tricep extension on bench. Best tricep mass builder. Slow and controlled.
  • Romanian Deadlift: Best hamstring exercise. Bar stays close to legs, hinge at hip, feel stretch in hamstrings.
  • Barbell squat (if you can rack safely): Use the bench as a rack alternative or do goblet squats with dumbbells.
  • Floor press: No spotter? Do floor press instead of bench. Safe, effective, slightly shorter range.

🔵 3–8kg Dumbbells

Perfect for isolation exercises and shoulder work. Don't underestimate light dumbbells — with the right techniques they build muscle effectively.

  • Lateral raise (3–5kg): Side deltoids. Shoulders are weak — even 3kg will be hard with correct form and slow tempo.
  • Front raise (3–5kg): Anterior deltoid. Keep slight elbow bend, raise to eye level.
  • Rear delt fly (5–8kg): Bent over 90°, arms out to sides. Crucial for shoulder health and rear delt development.
  • Dumbbell curl (full range): Supinate at top (twist wrist outward). Full stretch at bottom.
  • Hammer curl (8kg): Neutral grip. Hits brachialis — the muscle under your bicep that pushes the bicep UP and makes arms look wider.
  • Concentration curl: Elbow on inner knee. Maximum bicep isolation and peak contraction.
  • Dumbbell fly (on bench): Chest stretch and squeeze. Supplement to bench press. 5–8kg.
  • DB shoulder press (seated on bench): Full shoulder development. 8kg to start, progress to heavier backpack alternative.
  • Tricep kickback: 5–8kg. Extend arm behind body. Best for tricep long head.
  • Single-leg RDL (8kg): Balance + hamstring. One of the best posterior chain exercises.

📋 Push-Up Board

Most people ignore this tool. Used correctly it's a precision chest-shaping instrument that targets specific areas of your chest and triceps.

  • Yellow/inner (narrow grip): Maximum tricep activation. Close-grip push-up. Best for tricep size.
  • Blue (shoulder-width): Balanced chest + shoulder + tricep. Standard push-up.
  • Red (wide grip): Maximum chest activation. Mid-pec mass. Best "chest pump" position.
  • Elevated push-up (feet on bench): Decline position = lower chest and inner chest definition.
  • Deficit push-up: Hands on board (elevated), deeper range = more stretch = more chest activation.
  • Archer push-up: One arm wide, one arm close — intermediate one-arm push-up progression.
  • Weighted push-up: Backpack on your back. Progressive overload without any gym required.
Weekly target: 3 sets to failure every push session. When you hit 20+ reps on any variation → load the backpack or switch to harder variation.

🚴 Bike + Treadmill

Cardio during a bulk is strategic — not for burning fat, but for making you hungry, improving heart health, and aiding recovery.

  • Active recovery (20–30 min easy bike/walk): On rest days. Heart rate under 130 BPM. Flushes lactic acid, boosts blood flow to muscles, speeds recovery without adding training stress.
  • Pre-workout warm-up (10 min easy bike): Before lower body sessions. Perfect joint warm-up.
  • Hunger hack: Light cardio makes you hungrier — which helps you hit your 3,000 calorie target as a skinny guy who struggles to eat enough.
  • Sprint intervals (occasional): 8 × 30s all-out / 90s rest on bike. Builds explosive power and testosterone. Max 1× per week during bulk.
  • Treadmill incline walk: 15% incline, 5–6 km/h for 20 min. Extreme calorie burn without running stress. Good Saturday active recovery.
During a bulk: Keep cardio minimal and low-intensity. Max 2–3 sessions per week. You're trying to be in a calorie surplus — excessive cardio burns the calories you need to grow.

🔮 Gyro Ball + Grip Strengthener

These two tools work your forearms, wrists, and grip — often the limiting factor in pull-ups, rows, and deadlifts.

  • Gyro ball: 5–10 min daily. Works the wrist extensors and pronators/supinators. Builds forearm size (the part that shows with short sleeves) and prevents wrist injury under heavy loads.
  • Grip strengthener: 3 sets × 20 squeezes per hand, daily. When your grip gives out before your back on pull-ups or rows, you lose the training stimulus for the target muscle. Strong grip = better back workouts.
  • Dead hang (pull-up bar): 3 × max time. The single best grip + shoulder health drill. Decompresses spine, builds massive grip and forearm strength.
  • Farmer's carry (dumbbells): Walk 40 steps with the heaviest dumbbells you have. Grip + traps + core + calves. Whole body functional strength.
Use gyro ball while watching TV/YouTube — zero extra time cost, massive forearm gains over months.

🛒 What to Add Next (Budget Buys)

You already have an elite home gym. These small additions fill the remaining gaps:

  • Dip belt (~$30–50 AUD): For weighted pull-ups and dips. Once you can do 12+ reps of either, you MUST add weight to keep growing. Essential.
  • Resistance bands (~$20–30 AUD): Face pulls (shoulder health), band pull-aparts, warm-up, assisted pull-ups for beginners. Massive versatility.
  • Ab wheel (~$15 AUD): Best lower ab exercise. Roll-outs on knees → standing. Brutal and effective.
  • Heavier dumbbells 10–20kg (~$80–150 AUD): Your 8kg will become too light for rows, presses, and split squats within 2–3 months. Adjustable set is best value.
  • Foam roller (~$25 AUD): Daily recovery tool. Roll quads, hamstrings, thoracic spine after every session.
  • Gymnastic rings (~$40 AUD): Hang from power tower. Ring push-ups, ring rows, ring dips — exponentially harder and more effective than regular versions.
📊 Your Starting Point

You're an Ectomorph.
Here's the Full Truth.

🧬 Your Body Type

You're an ectomorph — fast metabolism, long limbs, naturally lean. The good news: you'll rarely look fat during a bulk. The challenge: your body burns through calories fast and resists weight gain.

At your age your natural testosterone and growth hormone are at the highest they'll ever be in your life. This is the single best window to build muscle. Guys who train hard in their teens build a foundation that lasts a lifetime.

From 1 push-up to 70 push-ups, from 50kg to 65kg+ — it's all possible within a year if you execute the system in this guide consistently.

⚡ Why You're Still Skinny

  • You think you eat a lot — you don't actually track it
  • You skip meals or only eat 2x a day
  • You eat clean foods that fill you up with low calories
  • You don't train with enough intensity (sets too easy)
  • You're inconsistent — great for 3 days, disappear for a week
  • You fear getting fat, so you under-eat
  • No structured programme — random workouts don't build muscle systematically

✅ The Only 3 Things That Matter

  • Calorie Surplus Every Day — Eat more than you burn without exception. This is the #1 reason skinny guys don't grow.
  • Progressive Overload Every Week — Force your muscles to do more than last time. More reps, more weight, harder variation.
  • Consistency for Months — 3 months of perfect execution beats 3 years of stop-start effort. The time passes anyway — use it.
🎯
Kevin's realistic targets at 175cm / 50kg: You have approximately 15–20kg of lean muscle available to build before reaching your natural potential ceiling. Year 1 goal = 60–65kg lean. Even gaining 10kg of muscle in Year 1 would be a physique transformation that will shock people who haven't seen you in a few months. With your home gym setup, you have better equipment than 80% of beginners at a commercial gym.
🍽️ Step 1 — Nutrition

Eating to Grow.
The Hardest Part.

Lifting is easy. Eating enough every single day as a skinny guy with a fast metabolism is the real challenge. This section gives you every tool and trick to solve it.

🔢 Your Personal Calorie Targets

Maintenance
~2,400cal

What your body burns daily at your size + activity level

Lean Bulk Target
2,800–3,000cal

+400–600 cal surplus = slow quality muscle gain, minimal fat

Protein Goal
130–150g/day

~1g per pound of bodyweight. 110 lbs = 110–130g minimum

Rate of Gain
0.3–0.5kg/wk

Slow = mostly muscle. Fast = fat + muscle mixed

If you're not gaining weight after 2 weeks: Add 200 cal/day. Keep adding until the scale moves. Your metabolism may be even faster than calculated. Don't be afraid to go to 3,200+ cal if needed.

The 5 Nutrition Strategies That Work for Ectomorphs

💧 Strategy 1 — Liquid Calories (The #1 Game Changer)

This single strategy is responsible for some of the fastest weight gains in skinny guys. Drinking calories bypasses the "too full" feeling because liquid leaves your stomach faster than solid food.

Kevin's Daily High-Calorie Shake (~900–1,000 calories):

  • 1 cup whole milk (~150 cal, 8g protein)
  • 1 large banana, frozen (~105 cal)
  • 2 tbsp peanut butter (~190 cal, 8g protein)
  • 1 scoop whey protein (~120 cal, 25g protein)
  • ½ cup rolled oats (~150 cal, 5g protein)
  • 1 tbsp honey (~60 cal)
  • 1 tbsp olive oil or coconut oil (~120 cal) — optional, no taste, huge calories
Total: ~895–1,015 calories · 46g protein
Blend oats alone first for smooth texture. Keep in thermal bottle. Sip across the day or drink post-workout when appetite is highest.
Habit stack: Make this shake immediately after every workout. Your appetite is highest post-training. Pair it with watching YouTube so it feels enjoyable. You'll look forward to it.

🥩 Strategy 2 — Calorie Dense Foods

Stop eating high-volume, low-calorie food. You need maximum calories in minimum volume to avoid feeling stuffed all day.

❌ Avoid (low cal density)✅ Use Instead (high cal density)
Chicken breast + plain saladBBQ chicken sandwich with cheese (700+ cal)
Fish + steamed vegSalmon pasta with olive oil (650 cal)
Plain rice bowlRice + olive oil drizzle + grated cheese
Apple for snackMixed nuts + 2 tbsp peanut butter (~400 cal)
Skim milkWhole milk (+60 cal per cup, same volume)
Large saladAvocado toast with eggs (400 cal vs 80)

Best calorie-dense foods to always have at home:

Peanut ButterMixed NutsWhole Milk Olive OilEggsOats AvocadoCheesePasta PotatoesBananasDark Chocolate

🕐 Strategy 3 — Spread 6 Meals Daily

Eating 3,000 cal in 2 big meals is brutal. Spread it into 4–6 smaller hits and it becomes manageable.
